baci for brū. paca, in the sense of explaining, optionally. There is aṇ. By the rule "before aṇ, o for the penultimate of baci," there is i: apacate. lip: alipata. sic: asicada. By the rule "for lip and the rest, optionally in the ātmanepada," there is aṇ: byalipta, byalipata, asikta, asicata.

By the sūtra of puṣ and the rest, there is aṇ in the form. puṣ and the rest are: gṛdhu, in the sense of longing, up to the end of the list. puṣ: apuṣat. śuṣ: aśuṣat. duṣ: aduṣat. śliṣ: these are the examples. svid: asridat. kṣudh: akṣudhat. śudh: aśudhat. ṣidhu: asidhat. radh: aradhat. tribi and tripi have been shown. amuhat, adruhat, asnuhata, asnihat. ṇaśu: naśu: "for naś and śami, before aṇ, e is to be stated": aneśāt, aśemata, adamata, atamata. śamu: aśramata, abhramat, akṣamat, akāmat, amadat. asu has been given as an example. yasu: ayasat, atasat, adasat. bhranśu: abhraśat. And so forth.

dyut and the rest: it is said that this very dyut and the rest also take the parasmaipada, so as to establish aṇ in the parasmaipada for dhut and the rest: adyutat, aśubhata, arucat, aśvitat, akṣubhat, abṛtat, asyadat. kṛpū: where r is heard, l is heard: aklipata. In the ātmanepada there is no an: byadyotiṣṭa, abartiṣṭa, abarttiṣṭa.

Those with the marker ḷiṭ: gamḷ: agamata. pṛsḷ: asibat. śakḷ: aśakata. mucḷ: amucat. By the rule "ghāsḷ for ad before san and the adyatanī," there is ghāsḷ: aghasata. lusḷ: ulubata. bidḷ: abidat. By the rule beginning "after saṃ, gam, ṛcch," it takes the ātmanepada. śābu: by the rule "it for the penultimate of śāsi before aṇ and a consonant," there is i. By the rule "and of śasi, pisi, and ghasi," there is ṣ: aśiṣat. jṝ and śvi have been shown. stabhu is a root that comes from the sūtra. By the explanation "optionally for jṝ, śvi, stambhu, mrucu, and mlucu," there is aṇ. The anuṣaṅga is elided: astabhat. Preceded by vi: by the rule "also after brate(?), after stambhi," there is ṣ: byaṣṭabhat, byaṣṭabhatām, byaṣṭabhan. mrucu and mlucu have the sense of going: amrucat, amlucat. glucu means stealing, and it is also used for going: aglucat. When there is sic: astambhāti, amocīt, amlocīta, aglocīt. After gruñc there is always sic: agluñcīt.

By the rule "after kuṭ and the rest, except before in, ic, and aṭ," guṇa is prohibited: akuṭīta, aghuṭīt, and so on. pad, in the sense of going: by the rule "after padi, ic in the first person singular of the ātmanepada," there is ic. The letter c serves to distinguish it in the rule "before in, ic, and aṭ, the penultimate a is long, and there is vṛddhi also of the nāmins." By this very rule the vowel is lengthened. By the rule "after ic, t is elided," the following letter t is elided: udabādi. In the dual and the rest there is only sic: udabatsāta. By the rule "after a dhuṭ, before a dhuṭ also," sic is elided. Before the unvoiced, the first letter is substituted: udapatthāḥ.

After dīb, jan, budh, pūri, tāyi, and byāyi, the affix ic is optional: adībi, adīpiṣṭa, adīpiṣātām, adīpiṣata. By the rule "and of jani and badhi," there is a short vowel: ajani, ajaniṣṭa. budhrir: budh: abodhi, abuddha. By the rule beginning "of a root whose third is the first and which ends in gh, ḍh, dh, bh," the first becomes the fourth, that is, the letter b becomes the letter bh: abhutsātām, abhutsata, abuddhāḥ. astūri(?), apūri, apūriṣṭa. atāyi, atāyiṣṭa, atāyiṣātām, atāyiṣātām, atāyiṣata, atāyiṣṭāḥ. abyāyi, abyāyiṣṭa, abyāyiṣātāṃ, abyāyiṣata.

By the rule "also in the impersonal and the passive," there is ic. By the rule "for roots ending in āt, āyis before ic," there is āyis: ādāyi, adhāyi. In the dual and the rest there is only sic. By the rule "for sthā and dā, e before the adyatanī in the ātmanepada," there is i: adiṣātām, adiṣata, adithāḥ, adhiṣātām, adhiṣata. When, in the sense of the impersonal and the passive, before its sic, the āśīḥ, and the śvastanī, it becomes like ic, from the statement "for vowels, han, grah, and dṛśi, iṭ is optionally to be stated as like ic," then: adāyiṣātām, adāyiṣata, asthāyi, asthīṣātām, asthīṣata, asthāyiṣātām, asthāyiṣata, anāyi, aneṣātām, aneṣata. iṭ is like ic.

anāyiṣātām, anāyiṣata. śriñ: aśrāyi, aśrāyaṣītām, aśrāyiṣata. ayāyi, ayāyiṣātām, ayāyiṣata. anābi, alābi, abābi, akāri, ahāri.

han: the penultimate is lengthened. By the rule "gh for the h of hanti before in and ic," the letter h becomes the letter gh: aghīni. iṭ is like ic: aghīniṣātām, aghāniṣata. Here, because the general word "optionally" states an arrangement, in the ātmanepada it is optionally changed into badhi: abadhi, abadhiṣātām, abadhiṣata. By the rule beginning "after hani, before sic, the ātmanepada is to be seen," the fifth letter is elided: āhaṣātām, āhaṣātām, āha ṣata.

grah: agrāhaṃ, agrāhiṣātām, agrāhiṣata. dṛśir: aderśi, aderśiṣatām, aderśiṣaha. By the rule "and before sic and the āśīḥ in the ātmanepada," there is no guṇa: adṛkṣatām, adṛkṣata. duh: adohaṃ, adhuṣātām, adhukṣata.

śamu: by the rule "not for those with iṭ ending in am, except bakami and cam," treatment like ic is prohibited: aśami, aśamiṣātāṃ, aśamiṣata. From yami, rami, and nami, because they have no iṭ, the penultimate is lengthened: ayūmi, arāmi, anāmi.

bhaj: abhāja. By the rule "after añji, before sic," the augment iṭ comes: āñji, āñjiṣātām, āñjiṣata. bhañj: abhañji, abhadkṣātām, abhādkṣata. By the rule "for radhi and jabh, before a vowel," the letter n is also the augment: arandhi. By the rule "not for radhi before iṭ, unless in the parokṣā," the letter n does not become the augment: aradhiṣātā, aradhiṣata. jabh: the letter n is the augment: ajambhi, ajambhiṣātām, ajambhiṣata. By the rule "for rabhi and labhi, when not before a vikaraṇa or the parokṣā," the letter n is the augment: prārambhi, prārapsātām, prarapsata. ḍulabhaṣ: by the rule "after labhi without a preverb, it is to be stated as optional," the letter n is optionally the augment: alambhi, alābhi. With a preverb, the letter n is always the augment: brālambhi.

By the rule "the agent of an action is like the object," the agent of an action becomes like the object. By the rule "and before the adyatanī, for roots ending in a vowel, optionally," the impersonal is optionally like the object: alāpi kedāraḥ svayameba, alabiṣṭa kadāraḥ svayameba, apābi yabarāśiḥ svayameba, apabiṣāya barāśiḥ svayameba, atāri kaṭaḥ svayameba, akṛta kaṭaḥ svayameba, ahāri bhāraḥ svayameba, ahrita bhāraḥ svayameba. duh: by the rule "before the adyatanī, for roots ending in a vowel, optionally," it is optionally like the object in the impersonal: adohi gauḥ svayameba. With saṇ: adhukṣata gauḥ svayameba. Because it belongs to kir and the rest, ic is prohibited after rudh: arudha gauḥ svayamiba. Because "the arrangement is optional" is a general rule, ic is prohibited by "not of tabi after anu."

Thus, in the Clear Words composed by the master Joyful Renown, the fifth chapter, setting out the domain of the adyatanī.

The parokṣā is to be stated. By this rule, "the parokṣā," there is the parokṣā. By the rule "iṭ is the augment at the beginning of a non-sārvadhātuka ending that begins with a consonant other than y," iṭ is the augment. From the restriction in the rule "only after sṛ, bibhṛ, stu, dru, śru, and sru, before the parokṣā," because only after sṛ and the rest is there no iṭ before the parokṣā, it is said that elsewhere the ending takes iṭ. By the rule "before caṇ, the parokṣā, the cekrīyita, and roots ending in san," the root is reduplicated. The meaning of the rule "the first consonant of the reduplicated syllable is to be kept" is that it is to be preserved, and the first is not to be elided. By the rule "short," the vowel is shortened.

Roots ending in ā. yā, in the sense of reaching: by the rule "after the letter a, au for aṭ," aṭ becomes au. By the rule "and before a diphthong," the letter ā is elided: yayau. By the rule "in what is not sārvadhātuka, ā is elided," the letter ā is elided: yatuḥ, yayuḥ. "Always ā before dyatbatī(?). For roots ending in a vowel, and for sṛj and dṛś, iṭ is optional before thal." Although iṭ would always be absent before trica, by the rule "if bribye(?), ad, and the rest, always iṭ before thal," iṭ is optionally the augment: yayātha. By the rule "before iṭ," the letter ā is elided: yayītha, yayathuḥ, yaya, yayau, yayiba, yayima. For the object, in the ātmanepada: yaye, yayāte, yayira, yayiṣe, yayāthe, yayadhve. Here the meaning is that, because the rule "after the root" has the earlier term as its name, it is not invariable. Therefore, saying that for these, with iṭ, the explanation is established, there is optionally dh: yayiḍhvi, yaye, yayibahe, yayimahe.

bā: babau, babatuḥ, babuḥ, babātha, babitha.

sthā: the root is reduplicated. By the rule "the unvoiced after ṣiṭ," the unvoiced letter after ṣiṭ is kept; the meaning is that ṣiṭ is elided. By the rule "the first and third for the second and fourth," the letter th becomes the letter t: tasthau, tasthatuḥ, tasthuḥ, tasthātha, tasthitha.

glai: by the rule "for roots ending in a diphthong, ā, when not before a vikaraṇa," there is ā. The c class in place of the k class: jaglau, jaglatuḥ, jagluḥ. In the impersonal: jaglau. mlau: mamlau, mamlatuḥ, mamluḥ. In the ātmanepada: mamli.

ḍudāñ: dadau, dadatuḥ, daduḥ, dade. ḍudhāñ: ḍhau, dadhatuḥ, dadhuḥ, dadhe.

cakṣiṅ: by the rule "optionally before the parokṣā," it is optionally changed into khyāñ. The root is reduplicated. The c class in place of the k class; the first in place of the second: ācakhyau, ācakhyatuḥ, ācakhyuḥ, ācakhye, ācakhyāte, ācakhyīre. Because it bears the marker ñ, it takes the endings of both voices: acacikṣe, ācacakṣāte, ācacakṣire.

Roots ending in e. ji, in the sense of conquering: by the rule "gi for ji before san and the parokṣā," it is changed into gi. The root is reduplicated. By the rule beginning "of a, the penultimate," there is vṛddhi. By the rule "āy for ai," it is changed into ay: jigāya. By the rule "and before the parokṣā," there is no guṇa. By the rule "for the letter i, not of a former reduplicated syllable, before a, when not a single letter," there is the letter y: jigyatuḥ, jigyuḥ. Before thal, eṭ is optional. There is guṇa and the change into ay: jigyitha, jegetha, jegyathuḥ, jigya. Before the latter aṭ there is optionally vṛddhi: jigāya, jigala, jigyiba, jiglima. In the ātmanepada, by the rule "in the ātmanepada, before all," there is no guṇa.

bijigye, bijigyāte, bajigyire, parājigye.

Ciñ, in the sense of heaping up. By the rule "for ci, optionally ki," it is optionally changed into ki: cikāya, cigyātuḥ, cikyuḥ, ceketha, cikayitha, cikyathuḥ, cikya, cikama, cikaya, cikyiba, cikyima; cicāya, cicyatuḥ, cacyuḥ, cicetha, cicayitha, cacyathuḥ, cicya, cicāya, cicyiba, cicyima. In the ātmanepada: cikyi, cikyāte, cikyire; cicye, cecyāte, cicyere, cicyeṣe, cicyāthe, cicyidhve. In one view there is ḍh: cicyiḍhve.

Iṇ, in the sense of going. It is reduplicated. There is vṛddhi of the root and the change into āy. By the rule "of the reduplicated syllable, before a dissimilar letter," it is changed into iy: iyīya. By the rule "of īṇ also," there is y. By the rule "of iṇ, a long vowel before the parokṣā when there is no guṇa," it is lengthened: īyatuḥ, īyuḥ, īyayitha, īyaitha, īyathūḥ, īya, īyāya, iyayi, īyiba, īyima. In the passive: īye, īyate, īyere.

Iṅ, in the sense of studying. By the rule "of iṅ, before the parokṣā," it is changed into gā. The letter ā is elided: adhijage, adhijagāte, adhijagire, adhijigaṣe.

Ik, in the sense of remembering. As before, the reduplicated syllable is changed into iy: abhīyāya. As with iṇ, so also for ik: adhīyatuḥ, adhīyuḥ, adhīyayiḥ, adhīyatha, adhīyathuḥ, adhīya, adhīyāya, adhīyaya, adhīyipa, adhīyima.

Kṣi: cikṣaya, cikṣimatuḥ, cikṣiyuḥ; because the former is a conjunct, there is iy. Cikṣayitha, cikṣetha, cikṣeyathuḥ, cikṣaya, cikṣāye, cikṣiya, cikṣiyiba, cikṣiyima. In the passive: cikṣeye, cikṣiyāte, cikṣire.

Ṣmiṅ: siṣmiye, siṣmiyāte, siṣmiyire.

Roots ending in ī. Ṇīñ, in the sense of leading to: nināya. By the rule beginning "for the letter i, y," there is y: ninyatuḥ, ninyuḥ, ninetha, ninayitha, nanyathuḥ, ninya, nināya, ninaya; ninyi, ninyāte, nanyire. Ḍīṅ: ḍidye, ḍiḍyīre. Līṅ: lilyāte, lilyire. Ḍukrīñ: cikrāla. It is changed into iy: cikriyatuḥ, cikriyuḥ. Hri: jihrāya, jihriyatuḥ, jihriyuḥ, jihritha, jihrayitha. Priñ: piprāya, papriyatuḥ, pipriyuḥ, pipritha, pipriyitha.

Roots ending in u. Cyuṅ: cucyupe, cucyubire, cucyubiṣe, cucyubidhva, cucyubiḍhva. Pruṅ: prabrube, pruprubāte, puprure. Yu, in the sense of mixing: yuyāba, yuyubatuḥ, yuyubuḥ, yuyabitha. Rurāba, rurubatuḥ, rurubuḥ, rurubitha. Nunāba, nunubatuḥ, nunubuḥ. Snu: suṣṇuba, suṣṇubatuḥ, suṣṇubuḥ. Kṣu: cukṣapa, cukṣubatuḥ, cukṣubuḥ. Kṣṇu: cukṣṇuba, cukṣṇubatuḥ, cukṣṇubuḥ. Ku, in the sense of sound: cukāba, cukabatuḥ, cukubuḥ, cukotha, cukapitha.

Roots ending in ū. Vuñ: lulābi, lulubatuḥ, lulubuḥ, lulabitha. Bāñ: pupāba, pububatuḥ, pupubuḥ, luluba, bubube. Bhū, in the sense of being. By the rule "for the final of bhū, b before the parokṣā and the adyatanī," the letter b is an augment. It is reduplicated. By the rule "short," it is shortened. By the rule "a for bhavati," the letter u of the reduplicated syllable becomes a: babhuba, babhubatuḥ, babhubuḥ, babhubitha, babhūbathuḥ, babhūba, babhūba, babhūbiba, babhūbima. In the impersonal: pubhūbe. In the passive: anubudhūbe.

Roots ending in ṛ. Hṛñ, in the sense of seizing. It is reduplicated. By the rule "j for h," there is the letter j. There is vṛddhi of the nāmin vowel: jahāra. By the rule "r for ṛ," there is r: jahratuḥ, jahruḥ. By the rule "after ṛ, before thal," iṭ is prohibited. There is guṇa: jahartha, jahrathuḥ, jahra. By the rule "optionally before the latter aṭ," there is optionally vṛddhi: jahāra, jahara, jahiba, jahima. Jahre, jahrāte, jahire, jahiṣe.

Dīṅ, in the sense of wasting away. By the rule "for dīṅ, the letter y at the end, before an initial vowel, when there is no guṇa," there is the letter y: upadidīyi, upadidīyāte, upadidīyire.

Deṅ and treṅ, in the sense of protecting. By the rule "for tayati, before the parokṣā, digi," it is changed into digi, and the reduplicated syllable is also elided: digyi, digyāte, digyire.

Smṛ. By the rule "also for ṛt with an initial conjunct," there is guṇa: sasmrara, sasmaratuḥ, sasmaruḥ, sasmartha. In the passive: sasmare, sasmārate, sasmarire.

Bhvi: dadhāra, dadhvaratuḥ, dadhvaruḥ. In the passive: dadhvare, dadhvarāta, dadhvarire. Hvṝ, in the sense of crookedness: jahvara, jahvaratu, jahvaruḥ.

Roots ending in ṝ. Kṝ: cakāra. By the rule "also of those ending in ṝt," there is guṇa. By the rule "before the parokṣā, for an a standing between single consonants, whose initial is unchanged," e and the elision of the reduplicated syllable would also apply, as in śaśa and dada; but this is to be blocked by the rule "not for those with initial b and those having guṇa": cakaratuḥ, cakaruḥ, cakarithi, cakarathuḥ, cakara, cakāra, cakara, cakāripa, cakarima. In the passive: cakarāre, cakarāte, cakarira.

Grī: jagāratuḥ, jagaruḥ. By the rule "optionally before a vowel," there is l: jagāla, jagalatuḥ, jegaluḥ. In the passive: jagare, jagale.

Śrṝ, in the sense of harming: śaśarā, śaśaratuḥ, śaśaruḥ. Dṛ, in the sense of tearing apart: bidāra, bidadartuḥ, bidadaruḥ. Sṛ: nipāpāra, nipaparatuḥ, nipaparuḥ. Some say, "for śrṝ, dṝ and prṝ, optionally short": biśaśratuḥ, biśaśruḥ, bidadratuḥ, bidadruḥ, nipratuḥ, nipabruḥ. Here śrā, drā and prā are also used in the sense of śrṝ, dṝ and prṝ. Then, by the rule "ā is elided before what is not sārvadhātuka," the letter ā is elided.

Now those without iṭ before the parokṣā are to be stated. By the rule "before the parokṣā, only after sṛ, bṛ, bhṛ, stu, dru, śu and sru," there is no iṭ. Sṛ, in the sense of going: sasāra, sasratu, sasruḥ, sasartha, sasrathuḥ, sasra, sasāra, sasara, sasṛba, sasṛma. Bṛñ: babāra, bibratuḥ. Bṝ: by the rule "for vṛ and those ending in ṝ, always iṭ before thal," there is always iṭ: babaritha. Babre, babrāte, babire, babhṛṣe. Bhṛñ: babhāra, babhratuḥ, babhruḥ.

Ṣṭuñ. By the rule "an aghoṣa following a ṣiṭ," the ṣiṭ is elided. Because of the conditioning cause there is ṣ, and after ṣ there is the ṭ class.

tuṣṭuba, tuṣṭubatuḥ, tuṣṭubuḥ, tuṣṭotha. Dudrāba, dudrubatuḥ, dudrubuḥ, dutrotha. Śru: śuśrāba, śuśrubatuḥ, śuśrubuḥ, suśrotha. Sru: susrāba, susrubatuḥ, susrubuḥ, susrotha.

Ḍukṛñ. By the rule "after kṛñ, when without suṭ," there is no iṭ: cakāra, cakratuḥ, cakruḥ, cakartha, cakrāthuḥ, cakra, cakāri, cakṛba, cakṛma. Cakre, cakrāte, cakṛde, cakṛṣe, cakrāthe, cakṛḍhve, cakre, cekṛbahe, cakrimahe.

Roots ending in a consonant with a as the penultimate. Paca. By the rule beginning "of a penultimate a, a long vowel," it is lengthened: papāca. By the rule "before the parokṣā, for an a standing between single consonants, whose initial is unchanged," there is e and also the elision of the reduplicated syllable: pecatuḥ, pecuḥ. By the rule "nityātvatāṃ," iṭ is optional: papaktha. By the rule "also before thal when it has iṭ," there is e and also the elision of the reduplicated syllable: pecitha, pecithuḥ, peca, babāca, pabaca, peceba, baciba, pecima. Peca, pecāte, paciṣe.

Ḍulabhaṣ: lebhe, lebhāte, lebhire. Rabhe: ārebhe, ārebhāte, ārebhīre. Nanāma, nematuḥ, nemuḥ, ninantha, namitha. Ṣaha: sehe, sehāte, sehire. Rama: reme, remāte. Birarāma, birematuḥ, peremuḥ, perararantha, biremitha. Ṇaha: nanāhi, nehatuḥ, nehuḥ, nanaddha, nehithaḥ. Tanu: tatāna, tenatuḥ, tenuḥ, tenitha. Śamu: śaśāma, śematuḥ, śamuḥ. Because of the words "whose initial is unchanged," it does not become e.

Kaṇa: cakāṇa, cakaṇatuḥ, cakaṇuḥ, cakaṇitha. Here too, since the word "also" is a general provision, in some cases the long vowel is also to be accepted.

By the rule "after radh, in the sense of harming," there is e, and also the elision of the reduplicated syllable: apararādha, abaredhuḥ, aparedhitha. Because of the words "in the sense of harming," it does not happen otherwise: apararādha, apararādhatuḥ, apararādhuḥ.

By the rule "for rāji, bhraji, bhrāśi and bhūśi, it should be stated as optional," there is e and also the elision of the reduplicated syllable: rarāja, rejatuḥ, rejuḥ, rarājuḥ, rejitha, rarājitha. Bhreje, byejāte, bhrājire, babhrāje, babhrājāthe, babhrājire. Bhreśe, bhreśāte, bhriśir, babhrāśate, babhrāśire. Bhleśe, bhleśāte, bhleśire, babhlāśe, babhlāśāte, babhlāśare. It does not apply to others. Kāśṛ: cakāśe. It is likewise before thal when it has iṭ.

By the rule "also for tṝ, phala, bhaja, trapa, śrantha, granthi and danbhi," there is e and also the elision of the reduplicated syllable. Tṝ: tatāra, teratuḥ, teruḥ, teritha. Phaphāla, phelatuḥ, pheluḥ, phelitha. Bhaja: babhaja, bhajatuḥ, bhejuḥ, babhaktha, bhejitha. Trapa: trepe, trepāte, trepire, trepiṣe. Śrantha: śaśrantha. By the rule "for indhi, śranthi, granthi and danbhi, before the parokṣā when there is no guṇa," the anuṣaṅga is elided: śrethatuḥ, śrathuḥ. Danbhu: dadambha, debhutuḥ, debhaḥ.

Jṝ: jajāra. By the rule "for jṝ, bhamu, trasi, svana, paṇa and syāma, it should be stated as optional," there is e and also the elision of the reduplicated syllable: jeratuḥ, joruḥ, jajaratuḥ, jajaruḥ, joritha, jajaritha. Bhrama: babhrama, bhrematuḥ, bhramuḥ, babhramatuḥ, babhramuḥ, bhremitha, babhramitha. Trasī: tatrāsa, tresatuḥ, tresuḥ, tatrasatuḥ, tatresuḥ, tresitha, tatrasitha. Svana: sasvīna, svenatuḥ, svenuḥ, sasvanatuḥ, sasvanatuḥ, sasvanuḥ, svenatha, sasvanitha. Phaṇa: phaphāṇa, phaṇtuḥ, pheṇuḥ, paphaṇatuḥ, paphaṇuḥ, pheṇitha, phapheṇitha. Syama: sasyāma, syematuḥ, syemuḥ, sasyamatuḥ, sasyamuḥ, syemitha, sasyamitha.

Because in most cases they necessarily occur together with the anuṣaṅga, it does not apply to those having the anuṣaṅga: śaśraṃthitha, jagranthitha, dadambhitha.

Dhvana: dadhvana, dadhvanatuḥ, dadhvanuḥ. Śasu, in the sense of harming. Dada, in the sense of showing. Ṭubama, in the sense of vomiting. By the rule "not for śaśa, dada, those with initial b, and those having guṇa," neither e nor the elision of the reduplicated syllable takes place: biśaśāsa, biśaśasatuḥ, biśaśasuḥ. Dadado, dadedāte, dadadire. Babāma, babamatuḥ, babamuḥ, babamitha. Bana: babāna, babānatuḥ, babanuḥ, babanitha.

Roots with ā as the penultimate. Ṭuyāṃcṛ: yayāca, yayāce. These are forms of both voices. Ślāghṛ: śuślāghra.

Roots with i as the penultimate. Miha: mimeha, mimihatuḥ, mimetuḥ, mimehitha. Ṣica: ṣiṣoca. Roots with i as the penultimate. Krīḍī, in the sense of abiding: ciḍikriḍi, cikrīḍituḥ, cikrīḍuḥ, cikrīḍitha.

Roots with u as the penultimate. Rudhir: rurodha, rurudhatuḥ, rurudhu. Mucli: mumoca, mumucatuḥ, mumucuḥ, mumocitha. Ruci: ruruci, rurucāte, rurucire.

Roots with ū as the penultimate. Kūja: cukūja, cukūjatuḥ, cukūjuḥ, cukūcitha.

Roots with ṛ as the penultimate. Bṛtu, in the sense of occurring: babṛte, babṛtate, babṛtire. Bridhu: babṛdha, babṛdhāta, babṛdhire. Śrīdhu: śaśridhe, śaśṛdhere. Kṛpū: where r is heard, l is heard: caklipe, caklipāte, caklibiro. Ṭubepri: bipepṛ, bibepe, bibipīte, bibepire. Ḍhaukṛ: ḍuḍhaukāte, ḍuḍhaukīre. Traukṛ: tutrauke, tutrekāta, tutraukire.

Now the suffix ām is to be stated. Cakāsṛ, in the sense of shining. By the rule "before the parokṣā, ām after cikās, kās and those ending in a suffix," there is the suffix ām. By the rule "after ām, kṛñ is to be appended," kṛñ is appended. It is reduplicated: cakāsīcakāra, cakāsīcakratuḥ, cākāsāṃcakruḥ, cakāsāṃcakartha. Cakleśa here stands as an example of what is not monosyllabic; therefore: daridrāṃcakāra. Culumpa is a root that comes from the sūtra: culumpacakāra. Kāsvī: kāsīcakre, kāsīcakrāte, kāsīcakriṣe. By the rule "for ām and the rest, optionally before what is not sārvadhātuka," the suffix ām is optional. Where gopāya stands, because it ends in a suffix, there is ām, and the a is also elided: gopāyīcakāra, dhupāyāṃcaṃkāra, bicchāyāñcakāra, paṇāyīcekre, panālāṃcakre. After kami there is iniṅ. There is guṇa.

kāmayāṃcikre. After riti there is also ṇī: ritīyāṃcakra. When it does not take the suffix ām, then, because it does not end in a suffix, there is no ām: jugoba, jugupatuḥ, jugubuḥ, dudhuba, bibiccha, peṇa, pene, cakame.

For ṛ there is a, and for a there is ā. By the rule "of an initial a, in all cases," it is lengthened. By the rule "after that, before an initial vowel, if the final is a conjunct, the augment n," as it applies, and by the rule "also for ṛ," the letter n is an augment: ānattta, ānṛtatuḥ, ānatttitha, ānṛtathuḥ, ānṛtaṃ, ānatti, ānṛtipa, ānaṃtima.

Daya, in the sense of giving. Aya, paya, maya, taya, caya and raya, in the sense of going. Ās, in the sense of sitting near. By the rule "also after daya, aya and ās," there is the suffix ām: dayāṃcakre, dayāṃcakrāte, dayācakrare. With parā prefixed: by the rule "of a prefix, before āyati," there is l: balāyīcikre. Āsīcakre.

Īkrā, in the sense of seeing. Sveha, in the sense of doing. Uha, in the sense of conceiving. Ubha and unbha, in the sense of filling. Ujja, in the sense of abandoning. Upja, in the sense of the sky. By the rule beginning "for one with an initial nāmin vowel and having a heavy syllable, other than ṛccha," there is the suffix ām: īkṣīcaka, īhāṃcakre, ūhāṃcakre, umjhāṃcakāra, ujjhāṃcakāra, upjāṃcakāra. Because of the words "with an initial nāmin vowel," where there is no initial nāmin vowel it does not take the suffix ām. Takṣa and tvakṣā, in the sense of making thin. Ava and rakṣa, in the sense of protecting: tatakṣa, rarakṣa. Whatever root never comes to have a heavy syllable has no suffix ām.

Iṣu, in the sense of wishing. Uṣa, in the sense of burning. It is reduplicated. The reduplicated syllable is elided. There is guṇa of the light penultimate. By the rule "of the reduplicated syllable, before a dissimilar letter," there are the changes into iy and uv: eyeṣe. A homogeneous vowel is lengthened: īṣatuḥ, īṣu. Ūboṣa, ūṣatuḥ, ūṣuḥ.

Ṛccha, in the senses of going, the faculties, decay, and bodily form. Because of the exclusion "other than ṛccha," ṛccha does not take ām. It is reduplicated. For ṛ there is a. By the rule "of an initial a, in all cases," it is lengthened. From the general rule "after that, before an initial vowel, if the final is a conjunct, the augment n," by the rule "also for ṛ," the letter n is an augment. By the rule "of ṛccha, for ṛt," there is guṇa: ānarccha, ānarcchatuḥ, ānarcchuḥ, ānarcchitha.

Uṣa, in the sense of burning. Bida, in the sense of knowing. Jāgṛ, in the sense of waking. By the rule "optionally after uṣa, bida and jagṛ," there is ām: oṣāṃcakāra. Because of the restriction "before ām, only of bid," there is no guṇa of bid before ām: bidāñcakara. There is guṇa: jagarāṃcakāra. Also uboṣa, bibee and jajāgāra. By the rule "before the parokṣā when there is no guṇa," there is guṇa: jajāgaratuḥ, jajāgaruḥ, jajāgartha.

Ñibhā, in the sense of fearing. Hri, in the sense of feeling shame. Ḍudhāñ and ḍubhṛñ, in the senses of holding and nourishing. Hu, in the sense of giving. By the explanation "for bhī, hī, bhrī and hu, also as before ti," there is the suffix ām, and its operation also proceeds as before ti. It is reduplicated: bibhayīcakāri, jihrayāñcakāra. By the rule "for bhriñ, hīṅ and māṅ, it," there is i: bibārīcakāra, juhapīcakāra. When there is no ām, there are vṛddhi and the change into āy: bibhāya. By the rule beginning "for the letter i, y," there is y: bibhyatuḥ, bibhyuḥ, bibhetha, bibhayitha. Jihrāya; it is changed into iy: jihriyatuḥ, jihriyuḥ. Babhār, babhratuḥ, babhruḥ. Juhāba, juhubatuḥ, juhubuḥ.

Now those whose penultimate is dropped are to be stated. Gamla. The penultimate a is lengthened. It is reduplicated. For the k class there is the c class: jagāma. Before forms without guṇa, by the rule "of the penultimate of gam, han, jan, khan and ghas," the penultimate a is elided: jagmatuḥ, jagmuḥ, jagamitha, jagantha, jagmathuḥ, jagma, jagāma, jagma, jagmiba, jagmima.

Hana is reduplicated. For h there is j. By the rule "also from the reduplicated syllable," for h there is gh: jaghāna. The penultimate is elided. By the rule "also of one whose penultimate is dropped," there is gh: jaghnatuḥ, jaghnuḥ, jaghanitha, jaghantha. In the passive, in the ātmanepada: jaghā, jaghāte, jaghnire, jaghīṣe.

Janī: jajñi, jajñate, jajñire. By the rule "when a dental class letter is joined with the c class or n, the c and ṭ classes," for n there is ñ. Khana: cakhāna, cakhnatuḥ, cakhnuḥ, cakhanatha.

Āṅ. By the rule "optionally before the parokṣā," it is optionally changed into ghasla: jaghāsa. By the rule "before the voiceless, the first of those that are not ṣiṭ," for gh there is k. By the rule "also of śāsi, basi and ghasi," there is ṣ: jakṣatuḥ, jakṣuḥ, jaghasitha, jakṣathuḥ. When there is no change into ghasla: āda, ādatuḥ, āduḥ. By the rule "for bṛ, bye and ad, always iṭ before thal," the augment iṭ is always added: ādetha.

Aṭa: āṭa, āṭatuḥ, āṭuḥ. Anju, in the senses of making manifest, bearing and going. It is reduplicated. By the rule "of an initial a, in all cases," it is lengthened. By the rule "after that, before an initial vowel, if the final is a conjunct, an augment," there is the augment n: ānañja, ānañjatuḥ, ānañjuḥ, ānañjitha. Aśū, in the sense of pervading. By the rule "also of aśnoti," there is the augment n. It is preceded by bi. There is y: byānaśe, byānaśāte, byānaśire.

Now those that undergo saṃprasāraṇa are to be stated. Graha. The penultimate a is lengthened. It is reduplicated. The remaining consonant is elided. By the rule "of both kinds, of the reduplicated syllable, before the parokṣā," there is saṃprasāraṇa. For ṛ there is i. For the k class there is the c class: jagrāha. By the rule "also before the parokṣā," there is no guṇa. When there is no guṇa, by the sūtra beginning with grahi there is saṃprasāraṇa: jagṛhatuḥ, jagṛhuḥ, jegṛhitha. Jagṛhe, jagṛhāte, jagṛhire.

Jyā. By the rule "of both kinds, of the reduplicated syllable, before the parokṣā," there is saṃprasāraṇa: jijyau. By the sūtra beginning with grahi there is saṃprasāraṇa: jaijyatuḥ, jijuḥ. Before thal too there is iṭ. By the rule "also before iṭ," the a is elided: jijyatha, jijyātha. Jajye, jijyāte, jijyire.

Piñ. By the rule "also of piñ, bayi," it is changed into paya. There is saṃprasāraṇa: ubāya. A homogeneous vowel is lengthened.

ūyatuḥ, ūyuḥ, ubayitha, uya. Ūyāte, ūyire. When it is not changed into payi, then by the rule "for pā and śvi, not even when there is no guṇa," there is no saṃprasāraṇa: pagau, babatuḥ, babuḥ. Babe, babāte, babire.

Byadha, in the sense of piercing: bibyādha, bibidhatuḥ, bibidhuḥ, bibyadhitha. Bibidhāte, bibidhere. Paśa: upāśa, ūśatuḥ, ūśuḥ, ubaśitha. Byaca: bibyāce, bibicatuḥ, bibicuḥ.

Because of the exclusion "of pracch and the rest, before the parokṣā," there is no saṃprasāraṇa: papraccha, papracchatuḥ, papracchuḥ, papracchitha, papraṣṭha, papracchathuḥ. Pracche, papracchire. Praśca: papraśca, papraścatuḥ, papraścuḥ, papraścathaḥ. Babraśce, babraścate, babraścire.

Bhrasja. By the rule "the third of the dhuṭ letters," for s there is d. By the rule "when a dental class letter is joined with the c or ṭ class, the c and ṭ classes," for d there is j: babhajja, babhrajjatuḥ, babhrajjuḥ, babhrajjitha. Babhrajja, babhrajjāte, babhrijjare, babhrijajaṣe.

Ñiṣvapa, in the sense of sleeping. There is aṭ. It is reduplicated. The penultimate a is lengthened. The remaining consonant is elided. By the rule "of both kinds, of the reduplicated syllable, before the parokṣā," there is saṃprasāraṇa: suṣvāpa. By the rule beginning "svapi, baci," there is saṃprasāraṇa: suṣupatuḥ, suṣubuḥ, suṣvapitha, suṣvaptha.

Bruñ. By the rule "for bru, baci," it is changed into baci: ubāca. A homogeneous vowel is lengthened: ūciṃtuḥ, ūcuḥ, upacitha, ūcathuḥ, ūca, ūbaca, ūciba, ūcima. In the ātmanepada: ūca, ūcvāte, ācare.

Yaja, bapa, and baha, peñ and byeñ,
hvayati likewise, bada and basa, and śvayati:
these nine are taught as the yaja group.

Yaja: īyāja, ījatuḥ, ījuḥ, īyajitha, īyaṣṭha. Īje, ījāta, ījire, ījiṣe. Ṭubapa: upāba, upatuḥ, upuḥ, upapitha, ubaptha. Ūpe, ūpāte, ūpere, ūbiṣe. Baha: upāha, ūhatuḥ, ūhuḥ, ūbahitha. By the rule "ḍh for h," there is ḍh. For the dental class there is the ṭ class. By the rule "for sahi and baha, o for a," there is also o: upoḍha, ūhathuḥ. Ūheta, ūhāte, ūhire.

Beñ. Because it ends in a diphthong, ā would apply, but this is blocked by the rule "not before the parokṣā of byayati." There is vṛddhi of the nāmin vowel, that is, for e there is ai. It is changed into āy. There is saṃprasāraṇa: saṃbibyāya. Saṃprasāraṇa applies only from the yaja group. By the rule beginning "for the letter i, y," there is y: saṃbibyatuḥ, saṃbibyuḥ. By the rule "for bṛ, bye and ad, always iṭ before thal," there is always iṭ: saṃbibyāyitha. Saṃbibyad, saṃbibyāte, saṃbibyire.

Hveñ. There is ā. By the rule "also of the reduplicated," there is saṃprasāraṇa: juhāba, juhubatuḥ, juhubuḥ, juhabitha, juhotha. Juhupe.

Pada: ubādaḥ, udatuḥ, uduḥ, upaditha. Ude, udāte, udāre.

Basa, in the sense of dwelling. By the rule "also of śāsi, basi and ghasi," there is ṣ: ubāya, uṣatuḥ, ūṣuḥ, ubasitha, upasa. Ūṣe, ūṣāte, ūṣere.

Ṭuośvi. Where the exclusion "for bā and śvi, not even when there is no guṇa" would apply, by the rule "optionally for śvayati," the procedure of saṃprasāraṇa is optionally carried out: śuśāba, śuśubatuḥ, śuśubuḥ, śuṣabitha. Śuśubi, śuśubāte, śuśubire. When there is no saṃprasāraṇa: śiśāya, śiśayatuḥ, śiśviyuḥ. Śiśvaye, śiśvayāte, śiśviyire.

Byatha. By the rule "also of byathi," there is saṃprasāraṇa of the reduplicated syllable: bibyathe, bibyathāte, bibyathire. Obyāyī. By the rule "for byāya, before the parokṣā, pi," it is changed into pi: ābibye, ābibyāte, āpibyire. By the rule "of the reduplicated syllable of dhuta, dhute and svāpi," there is saṃprasāraṇa: didhute, dedhutāte, didhutire, dedhutiṣe.

This is the sixth section, teaching the domain of the parokṣā, from the Clear Words composed by the master Joyful Renown.

Now the śvastanī is to be stated. By the rule "for the future, the bhaviṣyantī, the āśī and the śvastanī," there is the śvastanī. By the rule "before what is not sārvadhātuka and begins with a consonant other than y, the augment iṭ," there is the augment iṭ. Of these, some take iṭ. Some lack iṭ, because it is blocked by such rules as "after one ending in ā and monosyllabic, there is no iṭ": yātā, yātārau, yātāraḥ, yātāsi, yātāsthaḥ, yātāstha, yātāsmi, yātāspaḥ, yātāsmaḥ. In the passive, in the ātmanepada: yātā, yātārai, yātāraḥ, yātāse, yātāsāthe, yātādhve, ātāhe, yātāsvahe, yātāsmahe. It is the same for the other roots ending in ā: bhātā, lātā, śrātā, pātā, glātā.

Mīñ. By the rule "for mīnāti, minoti and dīṅ, where guṇa and vṛddhi stand," there is ā: mātā. Meñ: brāmātā. Dīṅ: dātā. Here, because of the words "from a monosyllabic root," iṭ occurs only after what is not monosyllabic. By the rule "of daridrāti, before what is not sārvadhātuka," the a is elided: daridrītā.

Roots ending in i. By the rule "after one ending in i, other than śri, śvi, ḍīṅ and śīṅ," iṭ is prohibited: bhetā, catā. With āṅ: adhyatā. Natā, kretā. Because of the exclusion "other than śri, śvi, ḍīṅ and śīṅ," iṭ does occur: śvayitā, śrayitā, ḍayitā, śāyitā.

Roots ending in u. By the rule "after one ending in u, other than yu, ru, ṇu, snu, kṣu and kṣṇu," iṭ is prohibited: hotā, svatā. Because of the exclusion "other than yu, ru, ṇu, snu, kṣu and kṣṇu," iṭ does occur: yabitā, rabitā, nabitā, snabitā, kṣabitā, kṣṇabitā. The roots ending in ū take iṭ. Bhū: bhabitā. Yūñ: labitā. Būñ: papitā.

Roots ending in ṛ. By the rule "after one ending in ṛ, other than bṛṅ and bṛñ," iṭ is prohibited. Ḍukṛñ: karttā. Hṛñ: hadttā. Ḍudhṛñ: bharttā. Because "monosyllabic" is the general condition, iṭ does occur after a root ending in ṛ that is not monosyllabic; therefore: jagaritā. Because of the exclusion "other than bṛṅ and bṛñ," iṭ does occur: kharītā, prabarītā. By the rule "after ṝt, and after bṛṅ and bṛñ, the iṭ is optionally long, except before the parokṣā and the āśiṣ," and by splitting the rule "it is not prescribed before the sic of the parasmaipada," it is optionally long.

parītā. Prā: parītā. The roots ending in ṝ take iṭ. Stṝñ: āstīritā. Gṝ: by the rule "optionally before a vowel," there is l: nigaritā, nīgalitā. "After ṛt, and after bṛṅ and bṛñ, it is optionally long": āstarītā, nigarītā.

Śaki: after k, iṭ is prohibited: śakṣa. By the rule "after paci, baci, bici, sici, rici and muci, from c," iṭ is prohibited. Baca: baktā. Baci: paktā. Baci: baktā. Sica: sektā. Rica: rektā. Mucḷ: moktā. By the rule "after brañci, from cha," iṭ is prohibited. By the rule "also of cha and śa," there is ṣ. After ṣ there is the ṭ class: praṣṭa.

By the rule "after yuji, ruji, rañji, bhuji, bhaja, bhañji, dya, bhrasja, yama, rja, priji, niji, biji and ṣvañji, from j," iṭ is prohibited. Yujir: yokṣa. Rujo: roktā. Ranaje: radktta. Bhujo: poktā. Bhaja: bhaktā. Bhanjo: bhāṃktā. Sanja: saṃktā. Tyaja: tyaktā. Bhrisja: by the rule "of bhṛja and the rest, ṣ," there is ṣ. By the rule "the first of a conjunct, also at the end of k and the like," the letter s is elided: bhraṣṭā. Here bhriji for bhrasji is given as an example: since it is the intended domain of the particle, it is explained as applying elsewhere than in saṃprasāraṇa. Because of these words, bhrasja is changed into bṛji: bhārṣṭā. After yaja there is ś, and also the ṭ class: yaṣṭā.

Ṭumarjo. By the rule "of marji and naśa, before a dhuṭ," the letter n is an augment. By the rule "of braści and masji, before a dhuṭ," the final is elided: madkta. Pṛja. By the rule "of sṛje and driśa, before a dhuṭ following a vowel, where guṇa and vṛddhi stand, the augment a," the letter a is an augment. By the rule "r for ṛ," there is r. There is ṣ. There is the ṭ class: praṣṭa. Nija: nektā. Bijir: baktā. Svānja: svadktā.

By the rule "after adi, hudi, kṣudi, svībyati, bidyati, bintati, banitti, chidi, bhidi, hiri, śidis, debadi, skenti and khiri, from d," iṭ is prohibited. Ada: attā. Tuda: tottā. Ṇuda: nottā. Kṣudir: kṣottā. Svida: svettā. Pida, in the sense of existing. Bidḷ, in the sense of finding. Bida, in the sense of discerning: battā. Bida, in the sense of knowing, takes iṭ: beditā. Chidir: chettā. Bhidir: bhettā. Hada: hattā. Śadḷ, in the sense of decaying: śattā. Ṣadḷ, in the senses of wandering, going and wearying: sattā. Pada: sattra. Skantir: skanttā. Khida, in the sense of being weak. Khida, in the sense of crushing: khettā.

By the rule "after rādhi, rudhi, krudhi, krudhi, panti, sru, śudhi, sidhyati, budhyati, byadhi and sādhi, from dh," iṭ is prohibited. Rādha: rādhā. Rudhir: roddhā. Krudha: kroddhā. Kṣudha: kṣoddhā. Bandha: bandhā. Śudha: śoddhā. Sidhu: siddhā. Budha, in the sense of understanding: boddhā. Because of the specification "after budhyati," iṭ occurs after bodhati. Budhir: bodhītā. Yudha: yoddhā. Byadhā: byaddhā. Sādha: sāddhā.

By the rule "after hani and manyeti, from n," iṭ is prohibited. Hana: hantā. Mana, in the sense of knowing: mantā. Otherwise: manitā. Because of the qualification "from n," iṭ is not prohibited after what does not end in n.

By the rule "after ība, tapi, tīpi, ṣvapi, bapi, śapi, cchupi, kṣibi, libi and sṛbi, from p," iṭ is prohibited. Āblḷ: āptā. Tapa and dhūpa, in the sense of burning; tapa is optional in the sense of lordship: tābtā. Tīpṛ: tebta. Ñiṣvapa: sraptā. Ṭubaba: bastā. Śapa: śaptā. Chupa: choptā. Kṣipa: kṣeptā. Lipa: leptā. Luplḷ: loptā. Pṛplḷ: barptā.

By the rule "after yabhi, dabhi and labhi, from bh," iṭ is prohibited. Yabha: by the rule "after gh, ḍh, dh and bh, except dhā, dh for t and th," for t there is dh. By the rule "the third for the fourths of the dhuṭ letters," for bh there is b: yapbhā. Rabha: rapbhā. Labha: lapbhā.

By the rule "after yami, rami, nami and gami, from m," iṭ is prohibited. Yama: yantā. Rama: dantā. Nama: nanta. Gama: gaṭtā.

By the rule "after riśi, ruśi, kruśi, liśi, piśi, diśi, driśi, yṛśi, mṛśi and danśi, from ś," iṭ is prohibited. Diśa and ruśa, in the sense of harming: reṣṭā, raṣṭā. Kruśa: kroṣṭā. Liśa, liśa: leṣṭā. Biśa: biṣṭā. Diśa: deṣṭā. By the rule "for bṛśiṅ, pṛji and dṛśa, before a dhuṭ following a vowel, where guṇa and vṛddhi stand, the augment a," the letter a is an augment: draṣṭa. Yṛśa: yāṣṭa, spaṣṭa. Mṛśa: mraṣṭa, maṣṭa. Dans: daṃṣṭa.

By the rule "after tviṣi, puṣyati, kṛṣi, śliṣyati, diṣi, piṣi, biṣi, śiṣi, tuṣi and duṣi, from ṣ," iṭ is prohibited. Tviṣa: tveṣṭā. Puṣyati: poṣū. Otherwise: poṣitā. Kṛṣa: by the rule "optionally for yṛśi and the rest," the letter a is optionally an augment: kraṣṭā, kraṣṭa. Śliṣyati: ślaiṣṭā. Dviṣa: dveṣṭā. Biṣḷ: beṣṭā. Biṣḷ: beṣṭā. Śīṣḷ: śaṣṭā. Śuṣa: śoṣṭa. Toṣṭa. Duṣa: daṣṭa.

By the rule "after pasati and ghasi, from s," iṭ is prohibited. Basa: bastā.

By the rule "after dehi, dihi, duhi, mihi, rihi, ruhi, lahi, luhi, nahi and bahi, from h," iṭ is prohibited. Daha: by the rule "gh for one with initial d," there is gh. By the rule "after gh, ḍh, dh and bh, except dhā, dh for t and th," there is dh. There is the third of the dhuṭ letters: dagdha. Dahi: degdha. Duha: dogdhā. Mihi: by the rule "ḍh for h," there is ḍh. There is the ṭ class. Before ḍh, ḍh is elided: meḍha. Riha: reḍhā. Ruha: roḍhā. Liha: leḍhā. Luhe: loḍhā. Rihi and luhi are roots that come from the sūtra; some do read them. By the rule "dh for nahi," there is dh. For t and th there is dh. There is the third of the dhuṭ letters. Naha: nadhā. Maha. By the rule "for sahi and baha, o for a," ḍh is elided and a also becomes o: poḍhā.

The others take iṭ. Phakka: phakketā. Śuca: śocatā. Rāji: rājitā. Bhrāja: bhrājitā. Aṇa: aṇitā. Raṇa: raṇitā. Kaṇa: kaṇitā. Dibu: debitā. Śibu: sebitā. Bṛtu: battītā. Bṛdhu: bardhitā. Graha: by the rule "after grahi, the iṭ is long, except before the parokṣā," it is long: grahvitā.

Now the optional iṭ is to be stated. Spṛ, in the senses of sound and affliction. Ṣu, in the sense of living beings giving birth from the womb. Ṣuṅ, in the sense of living beings being born. Those with an indicatory ū: gāhu, in the sense of stirring up. Takṣu and tvakṣu, in the sense of making thin.

By the rule "after svarati, sūti and sūyati, and after those with an indicatory u," the iṭ augment is optional. Svarttā, svāritā. Prasvatā, prasavitā. Sotā, savitā. Āvakāḍhā, avagāhitā. Taṣṭa, takyitā. Hvatā, tvakṣitā.

Guhū, in the sense of covering over. By "ha becomes ḍha," it becomes ḍha. It becomes dha. It becomes the ṭa class. Ḍha is elided before ḍha. With ni prefixed. By "the penultimate of gohi becomes ūṭ," the letter u becomes the letter ū. Nigoḍha, niguhatī.

Radha and the rest are to be stated. By "also after radha and the rest," the iṭ augment is optional. Radha is also in the sense of harming. Raddhā, radhitā. Ṇaśa, in the sense of not being seen. By "for masji and naśa, before a dhuṭ," the letter n is an augment. Naṃṣṭa, naśitā. Tṛpa: trabtā, tarptā, tarbitā. Dṛpa: draptā, darptā, darbitā. Druha, in the sense of wishing to do harm. By "see the explanation of druha, muha, snuha and sniha," it optionally becomes gha. Drogha. By "ha becomes ḍha," it becomes ḍha. It becomes ḍha. It becomes the ṭa class. Ḍha is elided before ḍha. Droḍhā, drohitā. Muha, in the sense of bewilderment. Mogdha, moḍhā, mohitā. Ṣṇaha, in the sense of vomiting. Snogdhā, snoḍhā, snohitā. Ṣṇiha, in the sense of joy. Snagdhā, sneḍhā, snehitā. Kuṣa, in the sense of drawing out. With nis prefixed. Because the word "also" has the sense of plurality, it becomes ṣa. From the exclusion "except for pati and niṣkuṣa," it follows that niṣkuṣa too optionally takes iṭ. Niṣkoṣṭā, niṣkoṣitā.

Iṣu, in the sense of desiring. Saha, in the sense of forbearance. Lubha, in the sense of attachment. Raṣa and riṣa, in the sense of harming. By "before ta, optionally, for iṣu, saha, lubha, ruṣa and riṣa," the iṭ augment is optional. Eṣṭa, iṣitā. By "the letter a of sahi and vahi becomes ot," ḍha is elided and it also becomes o. Sodhā, sahitā. Lobdhā, lobhitā. Roṣṭā, roṣitā. Reṣṭā, riṣitā.

Kṛ phū, in the sense of being able. Where it is not ātmanepada, there is no i before sya and san for vṛta and the rest. For the śvastanī forms too, it is only kṛbi that is excluded: from the wording "after kṛta and the rest, before sa, optionally, but not before sic," the iṭ is prohibited. It is so also in the parasmaipada. By "the r of kṛpi becomes l," it becomes l. Kalpā. In the ātmanepada: kalbitā. It is likewise in the impersonal and the passive.

When, in the sense of the impersonal and the passive, before sya, sic, āśiṣ and the śvastanī, by the rule "the iṭ of vowel-final roots and of hana, graha and dṛśa is optionally like ic," it becomes as though it were ic, there is the iṭ augment. Then there is the letter ā for those ending in āt, and vṛddhi for those ending in a nāmin vowel. The penultimate a becomes long. Dātā, dāyitā. Rātā, rāyitā. Sthātā, sthāyitā. Ciñ: cetā, cāyitā. Ṇīñ: netā, nāyitā. Hu: hotā, hāphitā. Ṣūṅ: sāvitā, savitā, sotā. Lūñ: lāvitā, lavitā. Kṛñ: kartta, karitā. Hṛñ: harttā, hāritā. Āṣṭaritā, āstāritā, āstāritā. Pṝ: paritā, vāritā. Śṝ: niśaritā, niśaritā, niśāritī, niśaritā, niśāritī. Hanta: by "the ha of hanti becomes gha before in and ic," it becomes gha. Ghānitā. Graha: grahītā, grāhitā. Dṛśi: draṣṭa, darśitā.

This is the seventh, the concise teaching on the domain of the śvastanī, from the Clear Words composed by the master Joyful Renown.

The āśīḥ is to be stated. By "for the future: the bhaviṣyantī, the āśīḥ and the śvastanī," it becomes the āśīḥ ending. By "e before the āśiṣ," it is the letter e. Dā: deyatā. Dhā: dheyāta. Dhā: dheyāta. Mā: meyāt. Gai: because it ends in a diphthong, it becomes ā. Gaiyāta. Pā: peyāt. Sthā: stheyāt. Ṣo: avaseyāt. Hā: heyāta. Glai: by "it is to be stated: optionally, where it is not in the position of an initial conjunct," it optionally becomes e. Gleyāt, glayāt. Mlai: mlaiyāt, mlayāt. In the ātmanepada: dāsīṣṭā, dāsīyastām, dāsīran. Dhāsīṣṭa, dhāsīyāstām, dhāsīran.

By "also in the āśiṣ in the parasmaipada," there is no guṇa. By "for nāmin-final roots, before yaṇ, āyi, in, āśiṣ, cvi, ci and krīyita, there is length before ya," it becomes long. Jīyāt, yaṃyāstāṃ, jīyāsu. Ciñ: cīyāt. Ṣṭuñ: stuyāt. Aṇi, in the sense of going. By "of in that has no prefix," it is long. Īyāt, īyāstām, īyāsuḥ. With si prefixed: samiyāt, udiyāt. From the wording "that has no prefix," it does not become long. Vijaṣāṣṭi, ceṣāṣṭī, stoṣāṣṭi. Briñ: śrayiṣāṣṭi. Urṇṇañ, in the sense of covering all over. Prauṛṇṇuyāt. Or else, the guṇa of udṇṇota: prauṛṇṇaviṣāṣṭi.

Roots ending in ṛ: by "before yaṇ and the āśiṣ, before ya," there is the augment of i. Kṛyāt, hriyāt, bṛyāt. By "also of those ending in ṛt," there is no guṇa. Kriṣīṣṭa, hrīṣīṣṭa, pṛṣīṣṭa. Stṛñ: by "ir for those ending in ṛt, where there is no guṇa," it becomes ir. The penultimate of r and v is long. Āstiryāta. By "for ṛt, vṛṅ and vṛñ, iṭ is optional before san, and in the ātmanepada also before sic and the āśiṣ; for ṛt with an initial conjunct, the expression is not established when suṭ stands outside," the iṭ augment is optional. By "also after ṛt, vṛ and vṛñ it is optionally long, not before the parokṣā and the āśiṣ, of īn," it is so. Length would be obtained from the split rule "in the parasmaipada it is not prescribed before sic," but it is prohibited by the words "the parokṣā and the āśiṣ." Āpariṣīṣṭa, pravariṣīṣṭa, ānāstariṣīṣṭa.

Roots ending in consonants. Paca: vacyāta, pakṣīṣṭa. Hana: by "hanti becomes vadhi before the āśiṣ," it is changed to vadhi. Vadhyata, āvadhiśiṣṭa. The parasmaipada forms of those with samprasāraṇa are to be stated. Gṛhyādlajaiyāt, vidhyāta, uśyāta, pṛcchatā, priścyuta, bhrijhyātā, supyāta, ucyāta, ijyāta. In the ātmanepada: grahiṣīṣṭa. By "for those with a nāmin vowel as penultimate, before sic and the āśiṣ, also in the ātmanepada," there is no guṇa. Bhitasāṣṭi. Budha: bhutsāṣṭi. Sṛkṣāṣṭi, dṛkṣāṣṭi. Those with iṭ: virtttaṣīṣṭa, virddhaṣīṣṭa. It is likewise in the impersonal and the passive.

Dāsīṣṭa, dāyiṣīṣṭa. Dhāsīṣṭa, bhāyiṣīṣṭa. Ceṣīṣṭa, cāyiṣīṣṭa. Badhaṣīṣṭa, ghāniṣīṣṭa. Grāhiṣīṣṭa, grāhīṣīṣṭa. Darśiṣīṣṭa, dṛkṣāṣṭi. From the wording "before sya, sīc, the āśīḥ and the śvastanī, in the sense of the impersonal and the passive, the iṭ of vowel-final roots and of hana, graha and driśa is optionally like ic," there is iṭ. "It also becomes like ic."

Thus, from the Clear Words composed by the master Joyful Renown, the eighth, the teaching on the domain of the āśīḥ.

The bhaviṣyantī is to be stated. By "for the future: the bhaviṣyantī, the āśīḥ and the śvastanī," it becomes the bhaviṣyantī ending. Roots ending in ā: yā, in the sense of attaining. Yāsyati, pāsyati. Dā: dāsyati. Dhā: dhāsyati. Roots ending in i: ceṣyati, niṣyeti. Roots ending in u: stoṣyati, lapiṣyati, lapiṣyati, paviṣyati, bhaviṣyati. Roots ending in ṛ: by "after han and those ending in riṭ, before sya," there is the iṭ augment. Kariṣyate. Śakli: because of the conditioning factor, it becomes ṣa. Śakṣyati. Paca: by "ki before a letter not homogeneous with the ca class," it becomes ki. Because of the conditioning factor, it becomes ṣa. Pakṣyati. Paca: pakṣyati. Praccha: by "also of cha and śa," it becomes ṣa. By "ka for ṣa and ḍha," it becomes ka. Because of the conditioning factor, it becomes ṣa. Prakṣyati. Daha: by "gha for initial da," it becomes gha. By "for the initial third consonant of roots ending in ghi, ḍha, dha and bha," and so on, the letter d becomes the letter dh. By "the first consonant, before voiceless sounds, for those that are not śiṭ," the letter gh becomes the letter k. It becomes ṣa. Dhakṣyati. Naha: by "dha for naha," it becomes dha. It becomes the first consonant: the letter dh becomes the letter t. Natsyāti. Sṛja: for sṛji and dṛśa, the letter a is an augment. Prakṣyati, drakṣyati. Budha: bhotsyati. For rabhi and labhi, the first consonant. Rapsyate, labsyate.

Those with optional iṭ are to be stated. Kṛtī, in the sense of encircling. Cvitī, also in the sense of harming. Ucchidir, in the senses of blazing and of playing. Utṛdir. Nṛti, in the sense of moving the body. Where it is not the ātmanepada, there is no iṭ before sya and san after pṛta and the rest. For the śvastanī it is only not after kṛpi. By "after kṛta and the rest, before sa, optionally, but not before sic," the iṭ augment is optional. Karttiṣyati, kartsyati. Certtiṣyati, certsyati. Chaddhiṣyatī, charttasyati. Tarddhiṣyati, nirttiti. Gama: by "after gaṃ, before sa, in the parasmaipada," there is the iṭ augment. Gamiṣyati. With saṃ prefixed: by "gaṃ after saṃ; ṛcchi, pracchi, spṛśu, vetti, artti and dṛśa," it takes the ātmanepada. Saṃgasyate. By "where it is not the ātmanepada, there is no iṭ before sya and san after pṛta and the rest," the iṭ is prohibited. It is so also in the parasmaipada. Vṛtu: varttsyati. Śridhu: śrartsyati. Syandu: santsyati. In the ātmanepada there is iṭ: varttiṣyati, varddhiṣyate, śarddhiṣryate, syandiṣyate. By "for the śvastanī it is only not after kṛphi," the iṭ is prohibited. It is so also in the parasmaipada. Kalapsyati. In the ātmanepada there is iṭ: kalbiṣyate. It is likewise in the impersonal and the passive.

When, by the rule "before sya, sic, the āśīḥ and the śvastanī, in the sense of the impersonal and the passive, the iṭ of vowel-final roots and of hana, graha and dreśa is optionally like ice," ice is made the affix, as though it were ice, then by "āyi for those ending in āt, before ic," it becomes āyi. Yāyiṣyate, yāsyate. Roots ending in i: jāyiṣyate, jeṣyate. Nālaṣyate, neṣyate. Roots ending in ū: hu: hāviṣyate, hoṣyate. Ghāniṣyate, haniṣyate. Graha: grāhiṣryate, grahīṣyate. Dṛśir: darśiṣyā, drakṣyate.

Thus, from the Clear Words composed by the master Joyful Renown, the ninth, the concise teaching on the domain of the bhaviṣyantī.

The kriyātibatti is to be stated. By "also those that have a past action," it becomes the kṛyātibatti ending. Yā, in the sense of attaining. By "there is no iṭ after a single-vowel root ending in āt," the iṭ is prohibited. Ayāsyata. By "after the letter i, śvi," and so on, the iṭ is prohibited. Aceṣyata, ajyeṣyata, alavaṣyata, avaviṣyata. By "after han and those ending in ṛt, before sya," there is the iṭ augment. By "aṭ at the beginning of the root, before the hyastanī, the adyatanī and the conditional," there is the augment. Hana: ahaniṣyata. Akariṣyata. In the impersonal and the passive, the iṭ is treated like ic, as before. Angāyiṣyata, ayāyiṣyata, agrāhiṣyata, adarśiṣyata.

Thus, from the Clear Words composed by the master Joyful Renown, the tenth, the concise teaching on the domain of the kṛyatibatti.

The san is to be stated. Guba, in the senses of hiding and of censure. Taji, in the sense of being sharp, and also in the sense of forbearance. Kit, in the sense of abiding, and also in the sense of curing illness. By "sana after gupti, ja and kita," it is in the sense of censure from hiding, and in the sense of forbearance from harming; likewise from tija. Also in the sense of doubt: in the manner of expression, san is explicitly stated after kati. The letter n serves to specify, in the rule "the letter i before san." Further, the root in general: the term "root" being applied, it means sārvadhātuka. Therefore, since it is not sārvadhātuka, the iṭ augment of the rule "the iṭ augment at the beginning of what begins with a consonant other than the letter y, when it is not sārvadhātuka" does not take place. By "also before a san that has no iṭ," there is no guṇa. By "before caṇ, the parokṣā, cekrīyita and san-final forms," there is reduplication. The initial consonant remains. By "the ca class for the ka class," the letter g becomes the letter j. Where jugupsa stands, titikṣa stands, and cikitsa stands, these are roots; since they bear the name "root," the suffix follows the root. By "vartamānā for the present," what is occurring is the vartamānā. Because they belong to ruca and the rest, the ātmanepada. A-n. The letter a and the procedure of sandhi. Jugupsahe, titikṣate. After kati, the parasmaipada forms: cikatsisti.

Māna, in the sense of worship. Badha, in the sense of binding. Dāna, in the sense of cutting up. Śānā, in the sense of sharpening. By "for māna, badha, dāna and śana, also the lengthening of the reduplicated syllable," there is sa. By "caṇ, the parokṣā," and so on, there is reduplication. The consonant remains. By "short," it becomes short. By "the letter i before san," it becomes a. After that, the reduplicated syllable becomes long.

Māna and badha take the ātmanepada endings: mimānate. By "for the root-initial third consonant of roots ending in gha, ḍha, dha and bha, the fourth, before dhra," the letter b becomes the letter bh. By "the first consonant, before voiceless sounds, for those that are not śiṭ," the letter dh becomes the letter t. Bībhassate. Dāna and śana take the endings of both voices. Dīdāṃsata, sādīṃsati. Śīśāṃsati, śīsañi, śīśaṃśāṃsaste.

Roots ending in āt. Pā, in the sense of protecting. By "after a root whose infinitive in tum is the object of desire, optionally, when the agent is the same," there is the suffix san. Pātumicchati: sisāsati. Sthātumicchati: taṣṭhāsati. Glai: because it ends in a diphthong, it becomes ā. Glātumicchati: jiglāsati. Mlai: maimlasati. Kai: cikāsati. Gai: jigāsati. By "before san, mi, mī, mā, dā," and so on, it is changed to is and the reduplicated syllable is also elided. By "ta for the s of sa, before what is not sārvadhātuka," it is the letter t. Mā, in the sense of measure. Med, in the sense of giving in return. Mitsati, sitsati. Dāṇa, in the sense of giving. Datsati. Do: ditsati. Deṅ and traiṅ, in the sense of protecting. Ditsati. Ṭudāñ, in the sense of giving. Ditsati, datsate. Cakṣiṅ, in the sense of speaking clearly. Cakṣiṅ becomes khañ. Ūkhyātumicchati: ācakhyāste.

From the wording "smṛ and dṛśi too, when they end in san; for ruca and the rest, śu, without āṅ and prati; and also of jña without anu, in the sense of cognition, as it is remembered," they take the ātmanepada. Jijñāsate, upajijñāsate. From the restriction in "of jñā without anu," when anu is prefixed it takes the parasmaipada. Anujijñāsati.

Hveñ: it becomes ā. By "also of the reduplicated," there is samprasāraṇa. By "for vowel-final roots, before san," it becomes long. Then there is reduplication. By "short," it becomes short. Juhusati. By "of dādi before san," it is the letter ā. Upadīdāste. Daridrā: by "the letter ī and ṛdhā," and so on, iṭ is optional. By "for daridrāti, before what is not sārvadhātuka," the letter ā is elided. Didariṣati. "Optionally" carries over like a leaping frog; and from the restriction "those earlier ones do not take place before a san without iṭ," the letter ā is not elided. Dadiridrāste.

Roots ending in i. Ji: by "gi for ji before san and the parokṣā," it is changed to gī. Jigīṣati, vijigīṣate. Ciñ: by "ki for ci, optionally," it is optionally changed to ki. Cikīṣati, cekīṣati, cicīṣati, cicīṣite. By "it should be stated: from he, when not before caṇ," it is changed to ghi. Jighīṣati. Ṭumiñ: by "before san, is for the vowel of mi, mī, mā, dā, rabha, śaka, bata and bara," it is changed to is. The reduplicated syllable is also elided. By "ta before sīsa, when not sārvadhātuka," the letter s becomes the letter t. Matsiti, matsiti. By "before the san of smiṅ, pūṅ, ri, la, añju, aśū, kṝ, gṝ, dṝ, dhī and pracch," there is the iṭ augment. Guṇa. Stismayiṣate. Śi, śri, tīṅ and śīṅ are those that take iṭ. Śiśvayiṣati. Śṛñ: by "optionally for the letter i, ṛ, bha, bhra, sja, danddhu, śri, yu, urṇṇu, bhara, jha, pisa, ni, tana, pati and daridā," the iṭ is optional. Śriśayiṣati, śiśīṣate, śeśrayiṣate. By "the letter i, and ridha," and so on, iṭ is optional before san. Jijñapayiṣati. Ṛ: by "īra and īt are to be stated for dhi and jñāpi," it becomes āti. The reduplicated syllable is also elided. Jñīpsati.

Roots ending in ī. Ḍīṅ: ḍiḍayiṣate. Śiṅ: śiśayiṣate. Ṇīñ: nanāṣiti. Ñibhī, in the sense of fearing. Bibhīṣati. Hrī, in the sense of shame. Jihrīṣati. Mid. Mīñ: optionally. By "before san, mi, mī," and so on, it is changed to is. The reduplicated syllable is also elided. The letter s becomes the letter t. Pramitsate, upamatsiti.

Roots ending in u. Hu, in the sense of giving. By "also after those ending in u," the iṭ is prohibited. Juhuṣate. By "for sunoti, before so and san," the change of the root's letter s to ṣa is prohibited. Susūṣati. Ru: dudūṣati. Yu, in the sense of mixing. By "optionally for the letter i, ṛdha, bhrasja, danbhu, śri, yu, ūrṇṇu, bharjña, visa, ni, tani and daridrā," the iṭ is optional. There is reduplication. By "for the letter u, before the letter a, when ja, a semivowel or the pa class follows," the u of the reduplicated syllable becomes i. Iyaviṣati, yuyūṣati, prorṇṇunaviṣati. Śru: from the wording "smṛ and dṛśi too, when they end in san; for ruca and the rest, śru, without ā and prati; and also of jñā without anu, in the sense of cognition, as it is remembered," it takes the ātmanepada. Śuśruṣate. From the restriction "without āṅ and brati," it does not take the ātmanepada. Āśuśruṣati, pratiśuśruṣati.

Roots ending in ūt. Bhū: bubhūṣate. Bū: by "smiṅ, pūṅ," and so on, there is iṭ. Pipaviṣate. But from būñ: pupūṣate, papūṣate.

Roots ending in ṛt. Kṛñ: by "wishing to do," there is sana. By "of those ending in a nāmin vowel that have no iṭ," there is no guṇa. By "for vowel-final roots, before san," it becomes long. By "ir for those ending in ṛt, where there is no guṇa," it becomes ir. By the penultimate of ra and pa, it is long. Cikīrṣati. Hṛñ: jihrirṣati. Cikirṣati, jihīrṣate. Ṛ, in the sense of going. By "smiṅ, pūṅ," and so on, there is iṭ. Guṇa. By "of the second, after an initial vowel," there is reduplication. Aririṣati. Dṛṅ, in the sense of respect. There is iṭ as before. Ādidariṣate. Dhriṅ, in the sense of perishing utterly. Didhariṣate. By "for ṛṅ, vṛṅ and vṛñ, iṭ is optional before san, and in the ātmanepada also before sic and the āśīḥ; for ṛt with an initial conjunct, the expression is not established when suṭ stands outside," the iṭ is optional. In the alternative without iṭ, by "for vowel-final roots, before san," it becomes long. By "ur also for a labial penultimate," it becomes ur. By the penultimate of ra and pa, it is long. Bubūrṣate, prābubūrṣati. When there is iṭ, there is guṇa. Vivarīṣate, praviviraṣati, prāvivariṣate. Ṭubhañ: the iṭ is optional, as before.

Bibhariṣate, bubhurṣataṃ, bibhariṣate, bubhāṣate. By "smṛ and dṛśi," and so on, it takes the ātmanepada. Susmurāte.

Roots ending in ṝt. Kṝ, in the sense of scattering. By "smiṅ, pūṅ," and so on, there is īt. Cikariṣataṃ. Gṝ, in the sense of pressing down. Jaṃgaroṣate. By "optionally before a vowel," it optionally becomes l. Jaṃgalṣiti. These are the ones listed together with pracchi. Kṝ, gṝ, dṝ and dhī: these are the ones with initial āt. Kṛñ, in the sense of harming. Gṝ, in the sense of sound. By "for ṛt, vṛṅ and vṛñ," and so on, there is iṭ. Cikariṣiti, cikīrṣati. Jigariṣati, jigīrṣati. Prāṃ, in the senses of protecting and of filling. By "for ṛt, vṛṅ and vṛñ," and so on, the iṭ is optional. Bupūrṣati. Tṛ: titīrṣiti. When there is iṭ, there is guṇa. Pipariṣati, tatariṣati.

Roots ending in consonants. Vaca: vivakṣati. Yaje: yiyakṣati. By "after those ending in san, as before," the endings are as before. Pipakṣate, yiyakṣate. Vasa, in the sense of dwelling. Sana. By "ta for the s of sa, before what is not sārvadhātuka," the letter s becomes the letter t. Vipatbati. Budha: by "for the root-initial third consonant of roots ending in gha, ḍha, dha and bha, the fourth, before sa and dhva," the initial becomes the fourth: the letter b becomes the letter bh. It becomes the first: the letter dh becomes the letter t. Pubhutsati. Rabha, in the sense of beginning. By "before san, mi, mī," and so on, it is changed to is, and the reduplicated syllable is also elided. By "also at the end, for the s and k of an initial conjunct," the letter s is elided. It becomes the first. Āripsata. Ḍulabhaṣ, in the sense of obtaining. Ālipsate. Śaklṛ: śakṣiti. Vatlṛ: by "the letter i and ṛdha," and so on, iṭ is optional before san. Pitsiti, vivatiṣati. Pada, in the sense of going. Patsite.

By "optionally for the letter i, ṛdha, bhrasja, danbhyu, śri, yu, ūrṇṇu, bharajña, visa, ni, tani, pati and daridrā," the iṭ is optional. Divu, which has an indicatory i: deviṣate. In the alternative without iṭ, by "śa and ūṭ for chva, also before the fifth," there is ūṭ. The letter ṭ serves to specify, in the rule "vṛddhi of ūṭ after the letter a." It becomes ya. There is reduplication. The consonant remains. Dudyūṣati. Śivu: from the restriction "for stauti and those ending in in, before ṣan," it becomes ṣa. Siseviṣati, susyūṣati. Tiṣṭhivu: tiṣṭhṛviṣati, tuṣṇyūṣati. Ṛdhu: by "of the second, after an initial vowel," there is reduplication. By "na, ba, da and ra, as the first of a conjunct, are not reduplicated, except before ya," the repha is not to be reduplicated. Adhidhiṣati. In the alternative without iṭ, by "īr and īt are to be stated for ṛdhi and jñapi," there is īra before svan. The reduplicated syllable is also elided. Īrtsati. Bhrasja: bibhrajjiṣate, bibhrakāti. By "bhṛji is here implied for bhrasji," it is changed to bhṛja. Bibharkṣati, bibharjiṣati. Danbhyu: didambhaṣiti. In the alternative without iṭ, by "also the iṭ of danbha," it becomes i. From the letters "also," it is also long. From the "also" in "also before a san that has no iṭ," in some cases there is no guṇa of the nāmin vowel. For one that has no indicatory it, the anuṣaṅga is elided. The fourth of the first. It becomes the first. Dhapsiti, dhipsāti. Ṣaṇu, in the sense of giving. Sisaniṣaṭi. In the alternative without iṭ, by "before a dhuṭ, for khani, sani, sani and jana," it becomes ā. Siṣāsati. Tanu: titaniṣati. In the alternative without iṭ: titaṃsati. By "it should be stated that titisati is optional," the penultimate is optionally long. Titīsati.

Anju: by "before the san of smiṅ, pūṅ, ri, anju, yaśu, kṛ, gṛ, dhri and praccha," there is iṭ. Anjijiṣati. Aśu: aśiśiṣate. Praccha: by "praṣṭumicchati," there is san. By "before the san of grahi, svapi and pracche," there is samprasāraṇa. Vipracchiṣati. Graha: by "before the san of graha and guha," the iṭ is prohibited. By "before the san of grahi, svapi and pracche," there is samprasāraṇa. By "ha becomes ḍha," it becomes ḍha. The fourth of the first. By "ka for ṣa and ḍha before sa," it becomes ka. Jighṝkṣate. Guhu: jughukṣati. Ñiṣvapa: by "grahi, svapi," there is samprasāraṇa. Suṣupsati. Jenī: jijaniṣaste. Yanu: yiyaṃsati. Ramu: riraṃsate. Nama: ninaṃsati. Gamne: by "after gama, before sa, in the parasmaipada," there is iṭ. Jigameṣati. By "gam after si, ṛcchi, pracchi," and so on, it takes the ātmanepada. From the wording "in the parasmaipada," there is no iṭ in the ātmanepada. Sajigisate. By "gami for iṇ and iṅ before san," it is changed to gami. Jigamiṣati, saṃjigaṃsate. If gamḷ is in the sense of understanding, it is not to be expressly stated with san. This one is likewise. I, in the sense of going. By "of the second, after an initial vowel," there is reduplication. By "for the letter a before san," it becomes i. Sarthānabratīṣiṣati. By "also of ik, like in," it is changed to gami. Adhijigamiṣati: iṅ is changed to gami. By "for the penultimate of hana and of the gama of iṅ," it becomes long. Adhijigāṃsate. Hana: the penultimate becomes long. By "also from the reduplicated syllable," it becomes gha. Jighāṃsatī. Aṅ: by "ghaslṛ for ad before san and the adyatanī," it is changed to ghaslṛ. The letter s becomes the letter t. Jighatsati. Daha: by "gha for initial da," it becomes gha. The first becomes the fourth, and it becomes ka. Diddhakṣati.

Vida, in the sense of investigating. Bada, in the sense of existing. Vidlṛ, in the sense of finding. Visitsati, vivitsati. Rudi, in the sense of shedding tears. By "before the san of ruda, vadi and muṣa," there is no guṇa. Rurudiṣati. Vid, in the sense of knowing. Divideṣati. Muṣa, in the sense of stealing. Mumuṣiṣate. By "of the reduplicated syllable of dyuti and svapi," there is samprasāraṇa. By "it should be stated: also san," guṇa is optional. Didyutiṣate, dedyotiṣate. Likewise: ruruciṣate, ruróciṣate. Liliṣiṣati, liloṣiṣati. Lulubhiṣati, lulobhiṣati. Śuśobhiṣati, śuśobhiṣate. Cukṣubhiṣate, cukṣobhiṣate. Opojī, in the senses of fearing and of trembling. By "of viji, before iṭ," there is no guṇa.

Vivijiṣate. By "for kuṭa and the rest, except before in, ic and aṭ," there is no guṇa. Ucchakuṭiṣati, utpupuṭiṣati. Muha: mumukṣati. Muca: mumukṣati. Because it belongs to kira and the rest, it takes the ātmanepada. By "it should be stated: optionally oṭ for muc when it has no object," it optionally becomes o. The reduplicated syllable is also elided. Mokṣate, mumukṣate.

Vṛtu, vṛdhu, śṛdhu and syandu: where these stand in what is not the ātmanepada, by "there is no iṭ before sya and san after pṛta and the rest; for the śvastanī it is only not after kṛbi; after kṛta and the rest, before sa, optionally, but not before sic," the iṭ is prohibited. It is so also in the parasmaipada. Vivṛtsati, śiśritsati, sisyastsati. In the ātmanepada there is iṭ: vivarttiṣate, vivarddhiṣate, śiśarddhiṣate, sisyandiṣate. Likewise kṛpū: ciklṛpsati, cikalpaṣite. Likewise krotī: cikṛtsati, cikarttiṣati. Cicritsati, cicarttiṣati. Cicchatsati, chicchardhiṣati. Tritritsati, tetardhiṣati. Nṛtī: ninṛtsati, ninirttaṣate.

Pṛja, dṛśir: sisṛkṣati. From the wording "smṛ and draśi too, when they end in san; after ruca and the rest, śru, without āṅ and prati; and also of jñā without anu, in the sense of cognition, as it is explained," it takes the ātmanepada. Dedṛkṣate. From the wording "where guṇa and vṛddhi stand," there is no augment of the letter a for sṛji and dṛśi.

Āslaṃ: by "of the second, after an initial vowel," there is reduplication. The ī of ābnoti. The reduplicated syllable is also elided. Āpsiti. Undi, in the sense of moistening. By "na, ba, da and ra, as the first of a conjunct, are not reduplicated, except before ya," it is not to be reduplicated. Undiṣati. Arḍa, in the sense of striving earnestly. Arḍiḍiṣati. Arccha, in the sense of worship. Arcchiciṣati. Ḍuyācvi: yiyīchiṣate. Smarddha, in the sense of true joy. Pisarbarddhiṣate. Krīḍī, in the sense of abiding. Cikrīḍiṣati. Kuja: cukūjiṣati. Ḍuvavṛ: vivebiṣate. Those with au as penultimate: ḍhaukṛ: ḍuḍhaukiṣate. Traukṛ: tutraukiṣate. Those with n as penultimate: sransu, dhvansu: sipṛ, siṣate; didhvisiṣate. Duha: dudhukṣāti, dudhukṣate. Liha: lalikṣate. Īśa: iśiśiṣate. Ās: āsisiṣate.

Likewise, in the impersonal and the passive there is yaṇ. The letter a is elided. Cikīrṣyate, jihīrṣyate. The others too are to be seen in the same way. Saptamī: cikīrṣeta. Pañcamī: cikīrṣatu. Hyastanī: acikārṣate. Adyatanī: acikīrṣita. Parokṣā: by "āma before the parokṣā, after cakāsa, kāsa and those ending in a suffix," there is āma. By "kṛñ is to be added after āma," kṛñ is to be added. Cikīrṣaṃcakara. Śvastanī: cikīrṣitī. Āśīḥ: cikīrṣita. Bhaviṣyantī: cikīrṣaṣati. Kriyātibatti: acikīrṣaṣyata. Thus it has been taught for all the domains.

The cekrīyita is to be stated. Yā, in the sense of attaining. When one wishes to express the sense of having attained in the past, or of attaining again and again, then by "the sound ya after a root, in the sense of the full repetition of an action, is the cekrīyita," there is the suffix ya. By "caṇ, the parokṣā," and so on, there is reduplication. By "short," it becomes short. By "long where there is no augment," it becomes long. Where lāyāya stands, these are roots, so they have the name "root." By "also after what ends in cekrīyita," it takes the ātmanepada. Yāyāyate. Vā: vāvāyate. Ḍudāñ: by "dā, mā, gā, yati," and so on, it becomes ī. By "guṇa before the cekrīyita," there is guṇa of the reduplicated syllable. Dedīyate. Likewise ḍudhāñ: dedhīyate. Mā: memīyate. Gai: it becomes ā. Jegviyate. Pā: bebīyate. Sthā: teṣṭhīyate. Ṣa, in the sense of ending. Seṣīyati. Ohāk: jehīyate. By "ī for ghrā and dhmā," it becomes ī. Jeghiyate, deddhiyate. Jyā: because it belongs to grahi and the rest, there is samprasāraṇa. Jejīyate. Byeñ: by "for svapi, syami and veñ, before the cekrīyita," there is samprasāraṇa. Bebīyate. Hveñ: by "also of the reduplicated," there is samprasāraṇa. Jehūyate. By "optionally for śvayati," there is samprasāraṇa. Śiśvīyate, śośūyate. By "for those ending in a nāmin vowel, before yaṇ, āyi," and so on, it becomes long. Cecīyate. Hi, in the sense of going. By "it should be stated: after hi, when not before caṇ," it is changed to ghi. Prajeghīyate.

Roots ending in i. Tīñ: nenīyate. Śīṅ: by "before ya, ī becomes aya," aya replaces the letter ī. Śaśayyate. Roots ending in u. Hu: jahūyate. Ṣṭuñ: toṣṭuyate. Yu: yoyūyate. Ru: rorūyate. Nu: nonūyate. Ūrṇṇūñ: from the general statement "of what has an initial consonant," the suffix ya does not extend to what has an initial vowel. By "also after ṛ and the rest," the suffix ya is to be established. Prorṇṇonūyate. Kuṅ, in the sense of sound. By "not for kavati before the cekrīyita," the change of the ka class to the ca class in the reduplicated syllable is prohibited. Krokūyate. From the specific statement "of kaveti," only the forms of kauṭṛ and kuvati take the ca class. Cakūyate. Luñ: lolūyate. Bopūyate.

Roots ending in ṛ. By "for ṛt, before cvi, the cekrīyita, yin and āyin, the ending īt," there is the augment of the letter ī. Cekrīyate, jehrīyate. Ṛ, in the sense of going. By "also after ṛ and the rest," there is the suffix ya. While "guṇa for arti and those with an initial conjunct" carries over, by "also before the cekrīyita," there is guṇa. Arāpyate. Roots ending in ṝ. Kṝ: ir for those ending in ṛt where there is no guṇa. The penultimate of r and v is long. Ceśīryate. Gṝ: with ni prefixed. By "for girati, before the cekrīyita," it becomes la. Nijigilyate. Pṝ: ur also for a labial penultimate. The penultimate of r and v is long.

Popūryate. Prī: popūryate.

Roots ending in consonants. Vac: vāvacyate. Yaj: yāyajyate. Paṭh: pāpaṭhyate. Vad: vāvadyate. Ñiṣvap: by "of svap, syam and vye, before the cekrīyita," there is samprasāraṇa, and it becomes ṣ. Soṣupyate. Sesimyate. Vaś, in the sense of being lovely. Since samprasāraṇa would apply by "grahi and the rest," by "of vaś, before the cekrīyita," samprasāraṇa is prohibited. Vāvaśyate. Cāyṛ: by "for cāy, kī before the cekrīyita," it is changed to kī. Cekīyate. Sphāyī and opyāyī, in the sense of growing. Pāsphāyyate. While "for cāy, kī before the cekrīyita" carries over, by "for pyāy, pī in the parokṣā," pyāy becomes pī. Pepīyate.

While "of khan, san and jan, before a dhuṭ" carries over, by what is explained as "optionally before ya," it becomes ā. Cākhāyate, sāsāyate, jājāyate. When there is no ā, then by "for those ending in a nasal, anusvāra at the end of the ā," there is the augment of anusvāra. Caṅkhanyate, saṃsanyate. Śamu: śaṃśamyate. Damu: dandamyate. Tamu: tantamyate. Gamḷ: jaṅgamyate. Ram: raṃramyate. Yam: yaṃyamyate. Nam: naṃnamyate. Han: jaṅghanyate. By "it should be stated that for hani, in the sense of harming, there is optionally ghnī," it is changed to ghnī. Jeghnīyate.

By "from those meaning going, only in the sense of crookedness," there is the suffix ya. Kramu: kuṭilaṃ kramati, caṅkramyate. Dram: dandramyate. By "from lup and the rest, in the sense of contempt," there is the suffix ya. Garhitaṃ lumpati, lolupyate. Śadḷ: śāśadyate. By "for car and phal, also u for the following a," there is the augment of anusvāra, and the following letter a also becomes u. The penultimate of r and v is long. Cañcūryate, pamphulyate. By "also of jap and the rest," there is the augment of anusvāra. Jañjapyate, dandahyate, dandaśyate.

Bhañjo, in the sense of crushing. For those that do not have i as an indicatory letter, where there is no guṇa, the anuṣaṅga is deleted. Bambhajyate. Paś: a root that occurs in the sūtra. Pampaśyate. By "for vañci, sraṃsi, dhvaṃsi, bhraṃśi, kasi, pati, padi and skand, nī at the end," there is the augment nī, and the anuṣaṅga is deleted. Vanīvacyate, sanīsrasyate, danīdhvasyate, banībhraśyate, canīkasyate, panīpatyate, panīpadyate, canīskadyate.

Bhid: bebhidyate. Budh: bobudhyate. Vṛtu, vṛdhu: by "rī for those containing ṛ," there is the augment rī. Varīvṛtyate, varīvṛdhyate. Dṛśir: darīdṛśyate. Jarījṛmbhyate, sarīsṛpyate, narīnṛtyate, śarīśṛdhyate, jarīgṛdhyate, parīpṛśyate, parīpṛcchyate. Ḍhaukṛ: ḍoḍhaukyate. Traukṛ: totraukyate.

Sūc, sūtr, sūtr, mūtr: because they belong to the cur class, there is in. By "also after ṛ and the rest," there is the suffix ya. By "of the kārita, before what is not ām, iṭ or a vikaraṇa," the kārita is deleted. Sosūcyate, sosūtryate, momūtryate. Aś: aśāśyate. Aṭ: aṭāṭyate.

In the impersonal and the passive, there is yaṇ. By "deletion also of a," the letter a is deleted. By "of ya, where there is no an," the letter ya is deleted. Pāpaṭhyate, bebhidyate. It should be seen likewise in every case.

Saptamī: lolūyeta. Pañcamī: lolūyatām. Hyastanī: alolūyata. Adyatanī: alolūyiṣṭa. Parokṣā: the augment comes from the very end of the suffix. Lolūyāñcakre. Śvastanī: lolūyitā. Āśīḥ: lolūyiṣīṣṭa. Bhaviṣyantī: lolūyiṣyate. Kriyātipattiḥ: alolūyiṣyata. Thus the domain of the cekrīyita has been taught.

The deletion of the cekrīyita is now to be stated. By the separate rule "its deletion before ac," the cekrīyita is deleted. By "some say carkarītam also," there are parasmaipada endings. From ad and the rest, an is deleted. By "optionally after the carkarīta," before a sārvadhātuka that begins with a consonant and takes guṇa, there is optionally the augment īṭ. Lolavīti, loloti, lolūtaḥ. By "both are abhyasta," it is given the name abhyasta. By "after an abhyasta, the n of anti is deleted," n is deleted. Loluvati. Lolavīṣi, loloṣi, lolūthaḥ, lolūtha. Lolavīmi, lolomi, lolūvaḥ, lolūmaḥ.

Vad: vāvadīti, vāvatti, vāvattaḥ, vāvadati.

Kruś: by "also not for the penultimate of an abhyasta" and "before a vowel in a sārvadhātuka taking guṇa," guṇa is prohibited. Cokruśīti, cokruṣṭaḥ, cokruśati.

Mud, in the sense of joy. Momudīti, momotti, momuttaḥ, momudati. Saptamī: momudyāt. Pañcamī: momudītu, momottu, momuttāt, momuttām, momudatu. By "after hu and a dhuṭ, dhi for hi," there is dhi. Momuddhi, momuttāt, momuttam, momutta, momudāni, momudāva, momudāma. Hyastanī: amomudīt, amomot, amomuttām, amomuduḥ. Adyatanī: by "after iṭ, also before īṭ," sic is deleted. Amomodīt, amomodīt, amomodiṣṭām, amomodiṣuḥ, amomodīḥ. Khādṛ: acākhādiṣṭām, acākhādiṣuḥ, acākhādīḥ.

Gṛdhu, in the sense of longing. By "rī for those containing ṛ," there is the augment rī. Jarīgṛdhīti. By "ri and r also when the cekrīyita is deleted," it also becomes ri and r. Jarigṛdhīti, jargṛdhīti, jarīgardhi, jarigardhi, jargardhi. Hyastanī: ajarīgṛdhīd, ajarīgṛdhīt, ajarigṛdhīt, ajargṛdhīt, ajarīghart, ajarighart, ajarghart, ajarīgṛddhām, ajarigṛddhām, ajargṛddhām, ajarīgṛdhuḥ, ajarigṛdhuḥ, ajargṛdhuḥ. With s: there is guṇa. By "also when deleted, before d and s," the first becomes the fourth, the letter g becoming gh. Because this is expressed by "before s at the end of a word" and so on, it becomes r. The r before r is deleted, and the preceding vowel is lengthened. Ajarghāḥ. Spardh: apāspāḥ. Kurd: acokūḥ. Rādh and sādh, in the sense of accomplishment: arārāḥ. Nardh: anānāḥ. Such forms and others are to be looked into. Thus the domain of the deletion of the cekrīyita has been taught.

In is now to be stated.

Yā, in the sense of reaching. Where someone else prompts the one who goes (yāti), that one becomes its cause. By this, and by "also from a root, in the sense of the cause," there is the suffix in. The letter n serves to distinguish it in "of the penultimate a" and so on. By "arti, hrī, vlī, rī" and so on, the letter p is augmented. Yāpayati. Because it ends in in, there are endings of both voices. Yāpayate. Ḍudāñ: dāpayate. Ḍudhāñ: dhāpayatām, dhāpayati, dhāpayate, sannidhāpayati.

Śo, in the sense of making thin. Cho, in the sense of cutting. Ṣo, in the sense of bringing to an end. Hveñ, in the sense of rivalry. Vyeñ, in the sense of covering. Veñ, in the sense of weaving yarn. Dheṭ and pā, in the sense of drinking. Because these end in a diphthong, there is ā. Niśāyayati, avacchāyayati, sāyayati, hvāyayati, vyāyayati, vāyayati. By "for śā, chā, sā, hvā, vyā, ve and pā, before in, y," the ā is changed to āy.

Pā, in the sense of protecting. By "for pāti, l at the end," the consonant l is augmented. Pālayati.

Smiṅ, in the sense of smiling slightly. By "for smi, ji, krī and i, before in," there is ā, and the letter p is augmented. By "for bhī and smi, before in, when the causal agent is meant," there are ātmanepada endings. Vismāpayate. Because smiṅ is stated for fear that comes from the causal agent, here, where fear comes from an instrument, this does not happen: kuñcikayā vismāpayati.

Ji, in the sense of conquering. Ḍukrīñ, in the sense of trading goods. Iṅ, in the sense of reading. There is ā, and the letter p is augmented. Jāpayati, krāpayati, adhyāpayati.

The in-form of bhī applies also to fear that comes from the causal agent, and because of the rule on ruc and the rest, there are ātmanepada endings. Muṇḍo bhāpayate: by "for bhī and smi, before in, when the causal agent is meant," there are ātmanepada endings. Because it is said "bhiṣi cinti," bhī also has a final ṣ. Muṇḍo bhīṣayate. Otherwise there are parasmaipada endings, with vṛddhi and the change to āy. Kuñcikayā bhāyayati.

Sphur, in the sense of radiating, with vi prefixed. By "vispharati," in the sense of the state, there is ghañ, and guṇa. By "for sphuri and sphuli, ā for o before ghañ," there is ā. By "he makes that," there is in. When the cause is to be expressed, there is in again. By "of the kārita, before what is not ām, iṭ or a vikaraṇa," the kārita is deleted. Visphārayati. By "he prompts one who is radiating," there is in in the sense of the cause. Visphorayati.

Cap, in the sense of continuing, and also of gathering. Cāpayati. Ciñ: cāpayati. Ṭuvap, also in the sense of begetting. Puro vāto gāḥ pravāpayati: pravāpayati is in the sense of begetting; the meaning is "makes them conceive."

Rādh and sādh, in the sense of accomplishment. Where it does not concern the world beyond: grāmaṃ sādhayati, annaṃ sādhayati. Where it concerns the world beyond: tapaḥ sādhayati, dharmaṃ sādhayati. Ṣidhu, in the sense of going: annaṃ sedhayati, grāmaṃ sedhayati. Also of one who accomplishes: tāpasas tapaḥ sedhayati.

Rā and lā, in the sense of taking. By "from lāti, also in the senses of deceiving and humiliating," there are ātmanepada endings. Kas tvām ullāpayate. Śyeno vartikām ullāpayati. This is because it is stated that, in the senses of deceiving and overpowering, lī is not used with the kārita.

Mīñ and druñ, in the sense of harming. Ḍumiñ, in the sense of throwing. Diṅ, in the sense of wasting away. By "for mā, mī, mi and dī, where guṇa or vṛddhi stands," there is ā, and the letter p is augmented. Māpayati, pramāpayati, upadāpayati.

Ṛ, in the sense of going. Hrī, in the sense of shame. Vlī, in the sense of covering. Rī, also in the sense of harming. Knūyī, in the sense of sound. Kṣmāyī, in the sense of trembling. By "for arti, hrī, vlī, rī, knūyī, kṣmāyī and those ending in ā, p at the end, with deletion of y and guṇa of the nāmin," the letter p is augmented. Deletion of y and guṇa of the nāmin also take place as is fitting. Arpayati, samarpayati, hrepayati, vlepayati, repayati, knopayati, kṣmāpayati.

Śṛñ: śrāpayati. Ṇīñ: nāpayati. Dhyañ, in the sense of moving. Prīñ, in the sense of satisfying. Dhūñ: by "for prīṇāti and dhūnoti, n," the letter n is augmented. Vidhūnayati, prīṇayati. Because it is specified as "prīṇāti and dhūnoti," here the letter n is not augmented: Dhū, in the sense of shaking; prīṅ, in the sense of joy. Vidhāvayati, prāvayati.
